(2) 긴 텍스트가 단어 중간에서 깨짐("최적화" → "최적"/"화") — 같은 원인. `body`에 전역 `word-break: keep-all`을 추가해 해결(`index.css`). 둘 다 접근성 트리·텍스트 콘텐츠는 정상이라 Testing Library/jsdom 테스트로는 절대 못 잡는다(위 "Vitest는 `css: false`로 돌아 레이아웃 버그를 구조적으로 못 잡는다" 항목과 같은 계열) — **한글(또는 다른 CJK) UI 작업 후에는 반드시 실제 브라우저에서 줄바꿈을 눈으로 확인할 것.**
- **북마크릿의 첫 관문은 로그인 상태가 아니라 출처(origin) 검사다 — "로그인한 상태에서 누르세요"는 틀린 안내다.** `frontend/src/lib/bookmarklet.ts:48`이 제일 먼저 확인하는 것은 `location.origin`이 blablalink인지이지, 유저가 로그인했는지가 아니다. 도움말이 "blablalink에 로그인한 상태에서 그 북마크를 눌러요"라고만 쓰면, 방금 우리 페이지에서 북마크 바로 링크를 드래그한 유저는 여전히 우리 탭(우리 origin)에 떠 있는 채로 그 문구가 시키는 조건("로그인한 상태")을 만족한 것처럼 보이고, 우리 탭에서 눌러 origin 검사에 실패한다 — 로그인 여부와 무관한 실패다. 문구는 반드시 "먼저 blablalink 페이지를 열고"를 포함해야 한다(`SyncHelp.tsx`, `SyncRosterPanel.tsx`의 `sync__hint`, 커밋 `86d0aad`).
- **레이아웃 수치는 재현 하네스가 아니라 실제 앱에서 재라 — 따로 조립한 CSS는 실제 캐스케이드와 다르게 잰다.** 동기화 도움말 패널의 높이를 서브에이전트가 컴포넌트 CSS만 따로 뽑아 재현해 측정했더니 788px이 나왔고, 그 수치를 근거로 스크린샷 폭 제한(`min(300px, 100%)`, 커밋 `86d0aad`)을 처방했다. 그런데 실제 앱(전체 스타일시트·폰트·기본 마진이 다 적용된 상태)에서 재보니 937px이었다 — 149px 간극 때문에 처방이 문제를 못 메웠고, 820px 뷰포트에서 URL 입력 칸(도움말 3단계가 가리키는 바로 그 칸)이 333px 아래로 밀려났다. 최종 해법은 `max-height: 55vh; overflow-y: auto`로 패널 자체를 뷰포트 안에서 스크롤 가능하게 만드는 것(커밋 `d0d1675`). 실제 렌더 트리 바깥에서 조립한 CSS로 잰 수치는 근사치조차 아닐 수 있다 — 레이아웃 버그는 실제 앱을 브라우저에서 열어 재라(위 "Vitest는 css: false로 돌아..." 항목과 같은 계열의 함정).
- **`position: sticky; top:`은 요소가 뷰포트보다 크면 목적을 못 이룬다 — `bottom:`으로 바꾸는 것도 오답이다.** 5덱 + 액션 행 컬럼이 ~760px인데 1366×768 화면에서 `top: var(--sp-4)`로 붙는 순간 버튼 하단이 776px에 걸려 **어느 스크롤 위치에서도 화면 밖**이다(`docs/decisions.md`, "덱 컬럼을 뷰포트 높이로 캡..."). `bottom:` 고정은 반대 방향으로만 붙는다(위로 스크롤할 때만) — 이 문제엔 안 맞는다. 해법은 컬럼에 `max-height: calc(100svh - var(--sp-4)*2)` + `display:flex; flex-direction:column`을 주고, 스크롤할 자식에 `flex:1 1 auto; min-height:0; overflow-y:auto`, 고정할 액션 행에 `flex-shrink:0`을 주는 것. **`min-height:0`이 없으면 flex 자식이 콘텐츠 크기 밑으로 안 줄어들어 `overflow-y`가 아예 안 걸린다.** `@media (max-width:900px)`에서 sticky를 `static`으로 끌 때 `max-height`도 같이 풀어야 한다(`b6bd04f`) — 안 풀면 좁은 화면에서 불필요한 스크롤이 남는다.
- **빈 문자열이 falsy 가드를 뚫고 "유효한 키"로 살아남을 수 있다.** `useBookmarkletImport`가 `open_id` 없는 동기화 페이로드를 `String(x ?? '')`로 삼켜 `profiles['']`를 만들었다(수신 검증 `isRawRosterPayload`가 애초에 `open_id`를 체크하지 않았다). `ProfileSwitcher`의 삭제 버튼이 `if (!activeOpenId) return`으로 "계정 미선택"을 가드했는데, `''`도 falsy라 그 프로필만 확인창조차 안 뜨고(계측: `confirm` 호출 0회) 삭제가 막혔다 — 하필 유저가 가장 지우고 싶어할 그 깨진 프로필이 지울 수 없는 유일한 프로필이었다. 고침: `null`만 "계정 없음"으로 취급하고(`activeOpenId === null`), 페이로드에 `open_id`가 없으면 프로필을 만들지 않고 그 자리에서 임포트를 거부한다(`5c35da8`). **일반화: "없음"을 나타내는 sentinel이 빈 문자열/0/NaN처럼 falsy와 겹치는 자료형이면, `!x`가 아니라 그 sentinel과의 명시적 동등 비교를 쓸 것.** 진단 단서: 실제 `open_id`는 32비트를 넘는 긴 정수라 JS 객체 키로 넣어도 정수 인덱스 최적화 대상이 아니라서 **삽입 순서가 그대로 남는다** — 재현할 때 짧은 숫자로 테스트 데이터를 만들면 객체 키 순서가 자동 정렬돼버려 드롭다운 순서가 실제와 달라지고 오도된다.
- **라벨을 여러 자리에서 통일할 땐 짝을 이루는 문구가 같이 움직였는지 반드시 확인하라.** 모드 이름을 바꾸면서(`3491e28`) 진행 배너("전부 최적화 중" 등)는 새 이름을 받았는데, 버튼의 로딩 라벨(`배분 중…`)과 네트워크 실패 메시지, 드래프트 섹션 헤딩은 옛 모드 이름에 그대로 남아 **한 화면에 같은 동작을 가리키는 이름이 둘 생겼다**(`67a9f10`, "Retired mode names finished leaking through loading labels, an error, and a legend"). 서브에이전트에게 작업을 나눠 맡기면 각자 자기가 건드린 파일만 보고 리뷰해 이 이음매(버튼 라벨 ↔ 배너 문구 ↔ 에러 메시지처럼 서로 다른 컴포넌트에 흩어진 같은 개념의 문구)를 놓치기 쉽다 — 이번엔 개별 작업 리뷰가 아니라 **전 브랜치를 다시 훑는 리뷰**가 잡아냈다. 문구를 바꿀 땐 같은 개념을 가리키는 모든 자리를 grep해서 짝을 확인할 것.
- **패널 레벨 스냅샷 하나로는 부족하다 — 모드 전환에서도 살아남는 결과가 둘
  이상이면 결과마다 스냅샷이 있어야 한다.** RecommendPanel에서 single(단일 덱
  평가)과 raid/draft 결과는 모드를 바꿔도 화면에 남는데, `evaluatedBoss`는
  패널 전체가 공유하는 상태 하나였다(제출마다 갱신). 그래서 다른 모드에서 다시
  제출하면, 화면에 그대로 남아 있는 **다른** 모드의 결과가 자기 것이 아닌 방금
  제출된 보스 프로필로 라벨(gimmick-unmet 뱃지)됐다. 고침은 `singleBoss`/
  `displayBoss`/`evaluateBoss`로 결과 슬롯마다 스냅샷을 분리하고, 각 슬롯은
  **자기 결과가 (재)계산될 때만** 갱신하는 것 — raid/draft의 캐시-히트·복원
  경로도 포함(`RecommendPanel.tsx`, 커밋 `e72f521` "Fix round 1: snapshot the
  gimmick-unmet boss per result, not per panel"). 일반화하면: "제출 시점 스냅샷"
  패턴(위 `submittedDraft` 항목들과 같은 계열) 자체는 옳아도, **그 스냅샷이 몇
  개의 독립적으로 살아남는 결과를 대표해야 하는지**를 먼저 세야 한다 — 결과가
  하나면 패널 레벨 상태로 충분하지만, 둘 이상이 서로 다른 생명주기로 화면에
  남으면 공유 스냅샷은 그중 하나를 잘못 라벨링한다.
- **무효화 축을 키에 넣어도, 저장된 값이 화면에 닿는 경로가 둘이면 둘 다 그 키를
  지나야 한다 — 한쪽이 우회하면 축은 없는 것과 같다.** `inputHash.ts`는 엔진 버전을
  캐시 키에 일부러 넣어 둔다(고쳐진 엔진이 옛 숫자를 계속 내주지 못하게). 그런데
  저장된 결과가 화면에 오르는 길은 둘이었다: `getCached(hash)`는 **방금 계산한**
  해시로 조회하니 엔진이 바뀌면 정확히 미스한다. `restoreResult`는 그 옆을 지나
  프로필의 `lastResultHash`를 곧장 꺼냈다 — **저장 당시의** 해시다. 키에 엔진
  버전이 있었지만 키를 아무도 안 물어봤다. `hold_burst_slugs`가 `RaidDeck`에
  추가되자(`821be39`) 그 전에 저장된 결과가 그대로 복원돼 `DeckCard`가
  `undefined.length`를 읽었다. 고침은 복원도 캐시 히트와 **같은 질문**에
  답하게 하는 것: 오늘의 로스터와 오늘의 엔진 버전으로 해시를 다시 만들어
  저장된 것과 일치할 때만 복원한다(`lib/restorableResult.ts`, 커밋 `19070f5`).
  새로 저장할 것은 없다 — 축은 이미 있었고 복원 경로가 안 쓰고 있었을 뿐이다.
- **렌더 중 throw는 패널 하나가 아니라 트리 전체를 언마운트한다 — 배경이 어두우면
  그 결과는 "검은 화면"이고, 원인을 앱 바깥에서 찾게 된다.** 위 항목의 버그가
  실제로 낸 증상은 "잠깐 보였다가 검게 변함"이었다(앱 배경은 `#0e0d13`). 에러
  바운더리가 없어 `DeckCard`의 예외가 전부를 걷어냈다. 이 증상이 오진을 부르는
  이유는 **바깥 신호가 전부 정상**이기 때문이다: 두 서버가 200을 주고, 같은
  Chrome 바이너리라도 저장된 결과가 없는 프로필에서는 완벽하게 그려진다. 그래서
  서버·GPU·확장·오버레이(RTSS)를 먼저 뒤지게 된다 —
  [[webview2-black-screen-is-rtss-injection]]의 진짜 RTSS 사례와 증상이 겹쳐서
  더 그렇다. **가른 것은 브라우저 콘솔 한 줄이었다**(`Cannot read properties of
  undefined (reading 'length') at DeckCard`). 검은 화면을 만나면 환경을 파기 전에
  콘솔부터 볼 것 — 그리고 재현이 안 되면 **내 프로필에만 없는 저장 상태**를
  의심할 것(여기서는 캐시된 추천 결과였다).
- **엔진 버전에 기대는 판단은 마운트 시점에 답할 수 없다 — `useEngineVersion`은 한
  박자 뒤에 도착한다.** 그래서 `restoreResult`는 첫 렌더에 `null`이고 나중 렌더에
  값이 된다. 복원 effect가 `[activeKey]`만 보고 있으면 그 값을 **영영** 못 받아
  복원 기능이 조용히 죽는다 — 스위트는 초록인 채로(누구도 이 타이밍을 테스트하지
  않았다). `[activeKey, engineVersion]`으로 두면 마운트당 두 번만 돈다(버전은 한
  번 정착한다). 일반화: 비동기로 도착하는 값에 의존하는 "한 번만 하는" effect는
  **그 값도 의존성에 있어야** 하고, 없을 때의 회귀는 에러가 아니라 **아무 일도
  일어나지 않음**이라 테스트로만 잡힌다.

## Backend / operations
- **An application logger's INFO records are silently dropped under uvicorn, and pytest's `caplog` hides this from a normal test suite.** uvicorn only configures its own `uvicorn.*` loggers; the root logger keeps its default WARNING level with no handler for application loggers, so a module-level `logging.getLogger(__name__).info(...)` emits nothing in a real server run even though the request itself succeeds (uvicorn's own access log still shows `200 OK`). A test using `caplog.at_level(logging.INFO)` forces the level for the duration of the test and therefore passes regardless — the telemetry looks tested while being dead in production. Found 2026-07-19 via `logging.getLogger('app.api').getEffectiveLevel()` returning 30 (WARNING) against a real run of the roster-sync endpoint. Fix: configure the application logger's own handler + level directly (`if not logger.handlers: logger.addHandler(logging.StreamHandler()); logger.setLevel(logging.INFO)`), not root — this leaves uvicorn's own access/error loggers untouched and doesn't double-log. A regression test that would actually catch this must exercise the real default-logging path (e.g. run the endpoint in a bare subprocess, no pytest/caplog involved) rather than a `caplog.at_level`-forced one. See `backend/app/api.py`, `backend/tests/test_assemble_roster_api.py`, commit `5069dc2`.
- **On Windows, `pkill -f` does not kill a stale server holding a port — verifying a fix against a live server first requires confirming the OLD process is actually gone.** A pre-fix server left running kept answering `200 OK` on the target port after a `pkill -f` that silently no-opped, which made a "verification" run against it look valid while testing nothing. Use `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort <port> -State Listen` to find the real PID and `Stop-Process` to free the port before restarting and re-testing.
- **`scripts/download_portraits.py`가 매니페스트를 통째로 재생성해 해소 불가 슬러그의 기존 항목을 삭제했다.** lootandwaifus HTML이 없는 유닛(ShiftyPad로 온보딩된 `laplace-ultimate-hero`, `maxwell-ordinary-mechanic`)은 포트레이트 경로를 유도할 수 없는데, 스크립트가 매니페스트를 빈 dict부터 다시 쌓다 보니 그 두 항목이 조용히 지워지고 있었다(발견: 2026-07-24, Sugar 온보딩). 새 유닛을 온보딩할 때마다(= HTML 없는 유닛이 새로 생길 때마다) 재발하는 구조적 버그다. 수정: 해소에 실패하면 기존 매니페스트에서 그 슬러그를 찾아보고, 아이콘 파일이 실제로 존재하면 보존해 `KEPT`로 로그에 남긴다(빈 dict부터 재생성하지 않고 기존 항목을 기반으로 갱신). 수정 후 diff가 sugar 2줄 추가뿐(기존 두 항목은 그대로)임을 확인해 검증했다.
- **A dead WebView2 browser process leaves the pywebview host process alive with zero signal — every "looks fine" check can pass while the window is blank.** WebView2 renders through a separate process group, not the Python process. If that browser process dies (measured cause: an overlay-hook injector, see below), the host process, the exit code, and the app log all stay clean — there is no crash to catch. Three independently-checked signals all read "normal" at the same time: the process is still in the process list, the backend port still answers 200, and opening the same URL in a regular browser works fine. **pywebview 6.2.1 does not even subscribe to WebView2's own `ProcessFailed` event**, so nothing in the library reports this either — you have to hook `webview.platforms.edgechromium.EdgeChrome.on_webview_ready` yourself and subscribe `sender.CoreWebView2.ProcessFailed` from inside it (import order matters: `Microsoft.Web.WebView2.Core` is only importable after `edgechromium` has attached the WebView2 assembly to the CLR). A failed `IsSuccess=False` init callback is the same failure mode with no `CoreWebView2` object to subscribe on at all. See `backend/app/desktop.py::guard_webview`, `docs/decisions.md` ("WebView2가 죽으면 앱 프로세스를 통째로 재시작한다...").
- **An overlay-hook injector (RivaTuner Statistics Server / MSI Afterburner) can silently kill WebView2's browser process, and there is no way to keep it from injecting.** The injected `RTSSHooks64.dll` faulted at the same offset (`+0x1490AF`, `0xC0000005`) in every captured crash dump (`%TEMP%\tmp*\EBWebView\Crashpad\reports`), and Chromium's own `third_party_modules` diagnostics named only that DLL. Injection happens before app code runs, so browser launch args don't dodge it — `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--disable-gpu` was tried and measured WORSE. Counts are **launches that ended with a working window**: 6/6 with RTSS off, 6/10 with RTSS on, 4/10 with RTSS on plus `--disable-gpu`, 10/10 with RTSS on after the restart-on-death fix. The failure is intermittent because it's an injection-timing race — this is what "reproduced once, then never again" during manual testing actually was. Since prevention isn't possible, the mitigation is process-level restart-on-death, not avoidance.
- **A `storage_path` shared across two pywebview instances doesn't error — it hangs the second instance's init forever, with no success or failure event.** Testing whether one WebView2 profile folder could serve all app instances: the second instance's WebView2 never rendered. Its initialization neither completed nor reported failure, so `on_webview_ready` was never called at all — a restart-on-death guard watching for `ProcessFailed`/`IsSuccess=False` doesn't catch this, because neither ever fires. The fix is one storage folder per instance, keyed by the (already-instance-unique) port. See `backend/app/desktop.py::webview_storage_dir`.
- **To test WebView2 crash-recovery, kill the browser process directly with `Stop-Process` instead of waiting for a real overlay-tool crash — it fires the identical `ProcessFailed`/`BrowserProcessExited` event.** This turns an intermittent, hard-to-trigger failure into an on-demand one for verification. One trap when checking "did the old window actually go away": the old process still shows up in the process list for **~2.5 seconds** after its own `os._exit()`, so checking sooner than that misreads a clean single-instance restart as "two windows."
- **문서 안내의 "우리가 보내는 요청은 이것뿐"이라는 주장은, 배포되는 앱이 실제로
  import하는 모듈 그래프를 subprocess로 물어봐서 지킨다 — 짐작한 파일 목록이
  아니라.** `backend/tests/test_privacy_claims.py`는 배포 앱이 실제로 거치는
  진입점(`app.api`/`app.desktop`/`app.updater`)을 새 서브프로세스에서 import한
  뒤, 그 결과 `sys.modules`에 실제로 올라온 `app.*` 모듈의 소스만 정규식으로
  긁어 URL을 화이트리스트(`CLASSIFIED`)와 대조한다. 같은 프로세스 안에서 하면
  `sys.modules`가 pytest 세션 전체와 공유돼, 형제 테스트가 미리 import해 둔
  `app.blablalink_api` 같은 — 배포 앱에는 없는 — 모듈까지 "배포 앱의 모듈"로
  잘못 센다(그래서 서브프로세스 격리는 장식이 아니다). 깨지는 조건은 "바깥
  요청이 늘었을 때"가 아니라 **분류되지 않은 URL이 하나라도 생겼을 때**다 —
  CORS 허용 출처든 독스트링의 출처 표기든, 사람이 "이건 나가는 요청이냐"에
  답해야 통과한다. 그 답이 "그렇다"면 안내 문구를 먼저 고쳐야 한다 —
  hosted-service 시절 "우리 백엔드가 보는 유일한 식별자"류 문구가 desktop
  전환 후에도 손으로는 안 고쳐진 채 남아 있던 재발을 막는 장치. 같은 파일이
  "로스터 조회는 앱이 아니라 브라우저가 한다"는 주장도 `app.blablalink_api`/
  `app.dotgg_client`가 `sys.modules`에 없음을 확인해 지킨다.
- **「부르면 받는」 질의 헬퍼는 이 저장소에서 채택률이 낮다 — 16개 중 2개.**
  `scripts/`에서 `evaluate_deck`/`simulate_raid` 결과를 소비하는 16개 파일 중
  `deck_search.never_full_bursts`를 실제로 부르는 것은 `sweep_slug_damage.py`·
  `measure_hit_rate_core_gain.py` 둘뿐이다(2026-08-08, `full_burst_passes.converged`가
  아무 하류도 없이 방치된 것을 확인한 세션). 놓치면 안 되는 신호에 헬퍼를 하나 더
  만드는 것은 「읽는 하류가 없다」는 문제를 이름만 바꿔 재생산하는 것이다 — 소비자가
  아무것도 안 해도 닿는 채널(아래 두 항목의 `warnings` + `pytest.ini`)을 먼저
  고려할 것. `docs/decisions.md`("고정점 루프의 수렴 실패는...") 참조.
- **`backend/pytest.ini`의 `filterwarnings = error`는 새 경고를 배선 0줄로
  전수 검사기로 바꾸는, 이미 배치된 인프라다.** 파이썬 `warnings.warn`을 하나
  추가하면 그 경고가 나는 모든 테스트가 실패로 뒤집힌다 — 약 2000여 개 테스트
  전부가 그 순간부터 그 경고의 소비자가 된다(수정 0줄). 앱 사용자에게는 안
  보여도 되지만 우리(스크립트·측정·테스트)는 놓치면 안 되는 내부 불변식 위반을
  알리는 저비용 채널로 재사용 가치가 있다.
- **경고 메시지에 가변 식별자를 실으면 파이썬 경고의 중복 접기가 무력화된다.**
  기본 경고 필터는 `(메시지 텍스트, 카테고리, 파일:줄)` 키로 중복을 접는데,
  메시지에 덱 슬러그처럼 요청마다 달라지는 값을 실으면 그 키가 매번 새것이 되어
  스윕 한 번이 수만 줄짜리 로그가 된다. 요청당 사실상 하나인 값(예:
  `fight_duration`)만 실어서 접히는 축을 지킬 것 — 그 값이 애초에 무엇을
  다르게 만드는지도 함께 알려준다.
- **"경고가 없다"를 단언하는 테스트에서 경고 클래스를 리스트 컴프리헨션 안에서만
  참조하면 공허한 테스트가 된다.** `[w for w in caught if
  issubclass(w.category, SomeWarning)] == []` 형태로 클래스 이름을 컴프리헨션
  **안**에만 두면, 잡힌 경고가 0개일 때 파이썬이 그 이름 자체를 한 번도 평가하지
  않는다 — `SomeWarning`이 오타·삭제로 존재하지 않아도 테스트가 통과한다. 실제로
  `FullBurstConvergenceWarning` 구현 전에 이 테스트가 이미 그린이었던 것을 보고
  잡았다. 카테고리를 컴프리헨션 **밖**에서 먼저 변수로 집어야 참조가 실제로
  평가된다. `backend/tests/test_interaction_soda_full_burst_extension.py::test_a_converging_run_stays_silent`.

## Data (lootandwaifus.com primary, dotgg fallback)

- **ShiftyPad serves its character list once PER LOCALE, so "the site is English-only" can be a reading of the wrong file.** The 2026-07-25 conclusion that blablalink carries no Korean names (Accept-Language, cookies and a `/ko/` path all returned 196 English rows) was true of the file the SPA requests and false of the data: the bundle builds the URL with `getLFormatLangUrl('/character/{l_lang}/nikke_list_{lang}_v2.json')`, substituting `{l_lang}` with the locale and `{lang}` with the language, **and stripping `_ko` when the locale is `ko`** — so Korean is `/character/ko/nikke_list_v2.json` while the intercepted one is `/character/en/nikke_list_en_v2.json`. Two traps sit on top of that. (a) The rule-shaped guess `/character/ko/nikke_list_ko_v2.json` also answers **200**, with a stale 154-row list — a wrong locale path fails as old data, not as a 404, so probing by status code alone "confirms" it. (b) Response interception can only ever show the locale the page is rendering; the question "does this data exist in another language" is answerable only from the path literals in the bundle (`grep getGameJsonResource`), which is also how `resource-url.js` was transcribed. `tools/collect-blablalink/korean-names.js` now joins that list onto `nikke-directory.json` as `name_ko` — no browser, no session — and the KR names are genuinely not derivable by transliteration (`Dolla` is 도라, `Moran` is 목단), so this is the difference between having the data and guessing.
- **"Is this stat already folded into the displayed value?" must be asked per source — ShiftyPad answers it differently for cubes vs. overload.** ShiftyPad's shown hp/atk/def already include the equipped cube's contribution (bare character stats if none is equipped), but shows overload option values SEPARATELY from those same stats, additive on top (both confirmed by Fienn — see `docs/decisions.md`, "ShiftyPad's displayed hp/atk/def already include the equipped cube"). Don't assume a UI's convention is uniform across every stat-contributing source it displays; check each one. This mattered far more for automated ingestion than manual entry: a human copying numbers by hand tends to notice something looks off, but a scraper swallows ShiftyPad's displayed numbers wholesale and would silently double-count (or drop) a source's contribution if the fold-in assumption is wrong.
- **NIKKE base stats (ATK/HP) are uniform across all characters of the same class (Attacker/Supporter/Defender) — only DEF varies per-character, and the engine doesn't consume DEF anyway.** blablalink's CDN serves each character's stat table as a `character_level_attack_list`/`_hp_list` array (level 1-1200); byte-for-byte diffing two Attackers (Rapi, Quency) showed the attack and HP arrays are identical, and likewise for Supporter (Mast) and Defender (Moran). Breakthrough/core coefficients (`grade_attack=20`, `core_attack=200`, `grade_hp=3000`, `core_hp=200`, etc.) are also identical across all four characters checked. Collectible-item stats are uniform per rarity tier (R/SR), and a favorite item (SSR) is fixed at "SR collectible at level 15" rather than its own stat curve. Cube flat stats are uniform regardless of cube type. **Practical consequence: a per-character stat manifest is unnecessary** — 3 class stat tables plus the shared breakthrough/core coefficients cover the whole roster (Phase B's collector design leans on this). See `data/blablalink-cdn/NOTES.md` (gitignored local data, not committed) and `docs/decisions.md` ("Phase B redefinition...").
- **The displayed-ATK assembly formula is NOT purely additive — a percentage term scales with base ATK, and it is only visible if you compare two levels of the same unit.** `data/blablalink-cdn/NOTES.md` drafted the formula as `base + grade*20 + core*200 + favorite + cube` (all additive); measured against the collected roster that under-explains ATK, and by a level-dependent amount. Rapi: Red Hood — base[Attacker][400]=90,318 vs measured 143,543 (residual 53,225); base[663]=322,224 vs measured 418,862 (residual 96,638). Solving `residual = F + r*base` across the two levels gives **r ≈ 18.72%, F ≈ 36,316**. Prime suspects for `r`: `grade_ratio=200` (the coefficient NOTES left as "용도 미확인" — plausibly 2%/grade, so 6% at grade 3) plus the affinity table's class `attack_rate` at `attractive_lv` 40 (~12.7%). The flat part F is consistent with 4 pieces of T10 gear (~22.6k) on top of core+grade+collectible+cube (13.7k). **Consequence: a single-level check cannot validate this formula** — a fit that looks perfect at one level silently mis-scales at another, so validate against both the `raid400` and `actual` stats the collector records per unit. Verify against all 159 collected units before trusting it; this two-level derivation comes from one unit.
- **Notes that live under `data/` are gitignored and therefore one `git clean` from gone — conclusions belong in committed docs.** The blablalink CDN stat research (class-uniform tables, breakthrough coefficients, the draft formula) existed only in `data/blablalink-cdn/NOTES.md`, invisible to git history and to anyone grepping the repo's committed docs. A later session re-derived it from scratch by live probing. Keep the raw dumps under `data/`, but write the *conclusions* into `docs/insights.md` where they survive and are greppable.
- **The solo-raid ATK formula, derived and measured against 159 units — 159/159 exact (closed 2026-07-19; an earlier 133/159 milestone is superseded, see the per-core-flat bullet below).** `backend/app/stat_assembly.py`:
  ```
  atk = base[class][level] * (1 + 0.02*grade) * (1 + 0.02*core)
        + core*core_flat_atk(class, corporation, resource_id) + grade*20   # per-UNIT, not a class constant
        + affinity[class][affinity_lv]                 # flat, despite the `_rate` column name
        + corporation_research_rank * 25               # account-wide, per corporation
        + per-slot gear                                # base * (1 + 0.3*own_corp + 0.1*level)
        + cube[cube_lv] + collectible                  # collectible: curve[lv], or curve[-1] for a favorite item
  ```
  Every piece was measured, not assumed. Two structural findings are easy to get backwards: the **breakthrough terms multiply** (grade 3 with core 1 measures 1.0812, not the additive 1.08) while the **gear bonuses add** (own-corporation gear at LV.05 is base*1.8, not base*1.3*1.5). Rounding is half-**up** on the bonus, applied before adding to base.
- **When a residual against ground truth won't close, divide it by the investment count before hypothesising a new additive term — discrete clustering of delta/count is the tell that the existing term's coefficient is wrong per-unit, not that a term is missing.** Closing Phase B2's last 26/159 ATK mismatches: the leftover deltas looked like noise as raw ATK, but `delta / core_count` collapsed them onto exactly 3-4 discrete values per class (Attacker: 118.95 standard / 142.90 PILGRIM / 132.95 OVERSPEC / 94.22 unexplained; Defender: 107.87 / 127.22 / 116.79 / 91.15; see `core_flat_atk` in `backend/app/stat_assembly.py`) — i.e. the per-core flat ATK constant was itself a hidden per-unit property, not a universal class constant plus a missing correction term. **A class default fitted to too few units can itself be the bug, not just imprecise:** the old `CORE_FLAT_ATK["Defender"] = 91` had been fitted to Nero, and Nero turned out to be one of the three "unexplained, measures less" outliers — so the wrong default was silently masking the correct one (107.87) for every OTHER Defender in the roster. Re-derive a class default from a large, deviation-free subset, and re-check it once outliers are identified, rather than trusting an early single-unit fit. See the design doc's "코어 flat은 클래스가 아니라 유닛 단위" section, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-18-stat-assembly-calculator-design.md`.
- **To find WHY two units of the same class behave differently, diff their full blablalink CDN character-stat JSONs key-by-key — don't assume the difference is in the base curve.** Rapi: Red Hood (core flat 132.95) vs Quency: Escape Queen (core flat 118.95, the class default) — same class, same rarity — were diffed field-by-field, surfacing `corporation_sub_type: OVERSPEC` (vs `None` for the ordinary unit) as the actual discriminator. The SAME diff simultaneously proved the base level-attack curve was NOT the cause: `character_level_attack_list` (1200 entries) was byte-identical between the two files, so whatever explains the ATK gap has to be in a coefficient applied on top of the curve, not the curve itself — turning a wide search into a targeted one. Files live in `data/blablalink-cdn/` (gitignored, not committed — see the worktree-sync insight above). Reusable pattern for any future "two same-class units disagree" puzzle: diff the full JSON before hypothesising, and treat an unexpectedly-identical sub-array as evidence ruling that piece out, not just a non-finding.
- **ShiftyPad's "Core Enhancement" stepper is a combined grade+core counter (0..10) whose consecutive deltas at level 400 give per-core flat ATK/HP directly and GEAR-INDEPENDENTLY, single-unit — an alternative to cross-unit fitting that verifies it without entangling the equipment model.** Discovered 2026-07-19, prompted by Fienn noting ShiftyPad has grade/core adjustment buttons alongside the level slider. On a single unit's page (`/shiftyspad/nikke?nikke=<resource_id>`), `div.upgrade-btns` with only −1/+1 whose parent text contains "Core Enhancement" steps 0→3 through limit-break grades (grade 0→3 at core 0) and 3→10 through core enhancements (core 0→7 at grade 3). Sweeping it at level 400 and reading the level-400 ATK/HP at each step: consecutive differences in the core regime cancel every core-independent term (equipment/affinity/cube/corporation), so `core_flat = (ATK[step10]-ATK[step3])/7 - base400*0.02*1.06` — no cross-unit fit needed. The grade regime independently recovers `grade_attack≈20`/`grade_hp≈3000`, confirming those table constants too. **Reading gotcha:** level-400 value = displayed value + delta, and delta can be POSITIVE (unit's real level is below 400) as well as negative (the common case) — a regex that only accepts `-?\d+` silently fails on positive-delta units (e.g. Vesti); use `[+-]?\d+` (same delta-sign fact as the jsdom stat-panel bullet above, now confirmed to bite the core sweep too).
  Swept 10 units and every one matched its cross-fit tier gear-independently: class (Brid Atk 118.97, Miranda Sup 113.52, Bay Def 108.08), PILGRIM (Scarlet Atk 142.97, Noah Def 127.36), OVERSPEC (Rapi:RH Atk 132.97, Anis Def 117.36), and the three "measured-not-explained" low outliers (Vesti 94.26, Rosanna 94.26, Nero 91.51). This resolved the OVERSPEC-Defender n=1 weakness — Anis alone gives 7 consistent core steps (~1394 ATK each) — and independently corroborated the unexplained-low trio as a real sub-class tier (Vesti and Rosanna both measure exactly 94.26; still unexplained WHY, but the value itself is solid, not a gear artifact). Also surfaced a genuine model-collapse: HP merges PILGRIM and OVERSPEC into ONE value (PILGRIM-Atk HP 6663.1 == OVERSPEC-Atk HP 6663.1), which is why `CORE_FLAT_HP` has one OVERSPEC tier while `CORE_FLAT_ATK` keeps them split.
  **Why the swept slope values were NOT swapped into `stat_assembly.py` despite confirming the tier ordering:** the slope runs ~0.2-0.57 ATK above the cross-fit for Supporters/Defenders (Attackers match near-exactly), because the cross-fit's `core_flat` and its equipment model are a SELF-CONSISTENT decomposition tuned together to reproduce the measured total — the equipment model has silently absorbed a small per-core bias. Swapping in the gear-independent slope value without also re-deriving the equipment model gear-independently would break the 159/159 parity (a core-7 Supporter would land ~1.6 ATK off, over the <1.0 tolerance band). Both decompositions produce the SAME final level-400 ATK, which is all the simulator consumes — so a full re-derivation buys at most ~4 ATK on a max-core unit (<0.003%) and was deliberately deferred. This sweep's value is **verification of the tier structure**, not a refinement to ship. Code ref: `backend/app/stat_assembly.py` (`CORE_FLAT_ATK`/`CORE_FLAT_HP`); the sweep itself was a live CDP probe, not committed to the repo.
- **Three values in blablalink's own stat tables do not mean what their names say — trust measurement over the schema.** (a) The affinity table's `attacker_attack_rate` is **flat ATK**, not a percentage: rank 40 reads 2340 in game and 2340 in the cell. (b) `recycle_research.attack = 25` is **per rank**, not a total: at rank 170 it contributes 4,250. (c) `stat_enhance.core_attack = 200` is **wrong for ATK entirely** — it is identical across all three class tables and reproduces no measured unit; the real per-core flat is a per-UNIT value (118.95 / 113.29 / 107.87 for a standard SSR Attacker / Supporter / Defender, higher for OVERSPEC units and Pilgrims — the earlier 119/113/91 reading was a class constant fitted to too few units, see the per-unit entry below), read off ShiftyPad's core screen where an Attacker gains 2,034 ATK per core at level 400 and 6,950 at 663, both leaving the same flat once the 2% step is subtracted. Each of these was found only because a unit refused to reconcile; the schema would have been believed otherwise.
- **A tid at level 0 is an empty slot, and a favorite item is priced at the collectible curve's maximum.** The collectible curve has a real entry at index 0 (3,029 ATK) but units whose `favorite_item_lv` is 0 measure no contribution at all, so `tid != 0, lv == 0` means nothing equipped — reading index 0 literally put 3,029 ATK on 26 units. Separately, a favorite item (애장품, the SSR upgrade of an SR collectible) always contributes `curve[-1]` regardless of its own level: four units holding one at level 2 each measure 9,688. **The tid block itself (2xxxxx vs 1xxxxx) is therefore the signature-ownership signal** that `SIGNATURE_OWNED` needed, closing a follow-up that had been deferred as "needs live verification".
- **The ATK flat term is account-wide progression, not just per-unit gear — and the in-game "추가 능력치" popup enumerates it.** Solving the flat residual analytically stalled until the popup showed its three sources by name: 호감도 RANK n, class rank (화력형/지원형/방어형), and corporation rank (엘리시온/미실리스/테트라/…). Captures kept at `capturedimages/` (see its README for the reading of each field). Each maps to a table already in hand: affinity contributes `affinity[att][<class>_attack_rate]` — **despite the `_rate` suffix that value is FLAT ATK, not a percentage** (RANK 40 attacker shows 2340, exactly the table cell; reading it as 23.4% is what made the numbers refuse to close). Class rank contributes HP only (`recycle_research` Class rows: attack 0, hp 750 **per rank** — RANK 176 → 132,000 HP). Corporation rank contributes ATK (`attack: 25` per rank — RANK 170 → 4,250). So: `flat = affinity_atk[class][att] + corporation_rank*25 + gear + cube + collectible + core*200 + grade*20`. Checked on the 23 ungeared units in the fixture: 18 land exactly (<1 ATK) assuming every corporation sits at rank 170; the five that miss are off by amounts consistent with their corporation being at a different rank (+500 = rank 190). Per-corporation ranks are per-account and readable from `GetUserProfileOutpostInfo.recycle_room_researches` (tid 1201-1205), so this is data we can fetch, not fit.
- **A cube record's `level1`/`level2`/`level3` arrays are cube-level → SKILL-level ladders, one per skill slot — not stats, and not indexed by cube level directly.** `tables.json`'s `resilience_cube` (id 1000303, formerly named `cube_sample`) stores, per cube level 1..15, the skill level each of the cube's three skill slots has reached (e.g. at Lv.15: `level1=3`, `level2=6`, `level3=0` — slot 3 never unlocks, so this cube has exactly two effects). Each slot's own percentage ladder lives separately, in `harmonycube_skill_group[slot].description_value_list[0].description_value`, indexed by THAT slot's skill level (1-based). **Slot position in `harmonycube_skill_group` maps to `level1`/`level2`/`level3` positionally, by convention — there is no explicit slot key in the data**, so slot N's skill level always comes from the Nth `levelN` array. A slot's percentage list runs past its own skill-level cap with values that slot can never reach (slot 1's list continues `29.69, 17.5, 20, 22.5…` after skill level 3, which is the cap) — this tail is what made an earlier session read the data as discontinuous and untrustworthy; it isn't discontinuous, it's just unreachable. See `cube_effects.py::cube_skill_percents` and `docs/decisions.md` ("Harmony cube modeled as a global...").
- **The CDN shard prefix cannot be discovered programmatically — read the request URL out of devtools instead.** blablalink's game tables live at `sg-tools-cdn.blablalink.com/<shard>/<hash>.json` where the shard is an opaque two-letter/two-digit token (`qe-66`, `zr-95`, `cp-03/vw-39`). Response interception finds them only if the SPA happens to request them, and for the equipment/affinity tables it never did in any flow reachable by script (cache disabled, fresh profile, cold storage, tab clicks, full `captureUnit`, IndexedDB reads — all zero). The filename hash IS visible in an earlier interception log, but without the shard the URL cannot be reconstructed. One devtools copy-paste from a human unblocked what ten programmatic attempts could not; the URLs are recorded in `data/nikke-stat-tables/tables.json` under `sources`.
- **The ShiftyPad overload value table is exactly linear in roll level — the previously-planned CDN "full overload curve" hunt is unnecessary for filling in levels.** Fitting a line through each effect type's observed endpoints reproduces every observed level to within 0.01pp (the table's own 2-decimal rounding): type 5 step 1.4014, 7 step 4.1093, 8 step 0.7043, 9 step 0.7046, 10 step 0.2936, 11 step 0.3407, 12 step 0.9800 (measured 2026-07-19 against `data/nikke-stat-tables/tables.json`). This is cross-checked, not merely smooth-looking: types 8 and 9 agree exactly at every level both were rolled at, and only 8 was ever rolled at level 15 — predicting 9's level 15 from 9's OWN endpoints alone gives 14.63, exactly 8's measured value. Consequence: the committed partial table already determines all of levels 1..15 by linear fill; a CDN snapshot (`collect.js --tables`) is only still needed to learn effect-type NAMES for types the roster never rolled — named so far: 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 (folded into the base stat panel: 6, 13; the rest are unnamed/unobserved). See `docs/decisions.md` ("Multi-user preconditions...") for where the linear-fill consequence was put to use.
- **A data correction is not verified until you check it changes the ANSWER — the level-400 fix moved alternatives 3-5 while leaving the top pick alone.** The collector shipped level-400 stats to fix a ~2.9x ATK inflation, but nothing had checked what that did to recommendations. Ranking the same 159-unit roster both ways (`scripts/verify_raid400_correction.py`, 57 mapped → 53 usable) gives: **top-1 deck identical**, **top-5 composition different** (level-400 promotes `maiden-ice-rose`; real-level promotes `dorothy-serendipity` and `ludmilla-winter-owner`), and top-1 damage inflated **2.91x** under the old convention — matching the 2.9x predicted in `docs/decisions.md`. So the pre-collector headline recommendation was never wrong, but its alternatives and every absolute damage figure were. Note the reordering is NOT explained by relative investment: all five units scale 2.82-2.95x between the two levels, and `maiden-ice-rose` has the *lowest* level-400 ATK of the group yet still places top-5 — the cause is the deck model's non-linearity, not per-unit ATK order, and it was left unexplained rather than guessed at.
- **Solo Raid is level-400-normalized — the deck-builder must use level-400 stats, not the user's actual account level.** ShiftyPad's main stat display shows the character's actual-level value plus a level-slider delta; the level-400 number is only obtained as (actual + delta at slider=400), never displayed as a clean standalone figure. Confirmed by measurement: the current manual-entry convention (actual level, e.g. 663) gives ATK ~2.9x too high for Solo Raid (Rapi: 418,862 actual vs 143,543 at level 400). See `docs/decisions.md` ("Phase B redefinition...").
- **Neither the ExiaInvasion export nor the authenticated raw API return the one number Phase A/B actually need: final assembled ATK.** The export carries skills/breakthrough/overload rolls but no HP/ATK/DEF and no per-character equipped cube (its `cubes` key is only the account-wide inventory). The authenticated raw API (`GetUserCharacterDetails`) goes further — armor tier/level, cube, skills — but still never returns the final computed ATK; only ShiftyPad computes and displays that, client-side. This is why Phase B's collector reads ShiftyPad's DOM rather than either data source. See `docs/decisions.md` ("Phase A: ExiaInvasion JSON roster import...", "Phase B redefinition...").
- **CDP (`--remote-debugging`) attach to an already-logged-in browser is how a personal tool reaches session-gated blablalink data without storing credentials.** Since ShiftyPad/blablalink don't expose a token-based API for this use case, the Phase B collector attaches to Fienn's own logged-in Chrome via Chrome DevTools Protocol rather than persisting a cookie/token — the browser's own session is the credential store. A per-Nikke ShiftyPad page is reached at `/shiftyspad/nikke?nikke=<resource_id>`, where `resource_id` is available both in blablalink's character-directory data and in the ExiaInvasion export.
- **`data/` is gitignored, so a git worktree does NOT get it — any coverage or loadability measurement taken in a worktree is a false negative until you sync.** Encoding Ein (2026-07-17), a probe said she had no dotgg weapon-stat file, so she was written up as "encoded but not API-loadable, no honest source for her weapon stats". Fienn pointed out `data/dotgg/char_ein.json` plainly exists and her dotgg page is live — she shipped well before the 2026-05 shutdown. The worktree's `data/dotgg/` had 53 files against main's 71: `data/dotgg/` and `data/lootandwaifus/` are in `.gitignore`, and git only ever populates a new worktree from TRACKED files. The 18 missing ones had been collected in main after this worktree was cut. Syncing (`cp -n ../../../data/dotgg/*.json data/dotgg/`) flipped loadable from 50/58 to 54/58 — ein, ark-ranger-black, prika and marciana-marine-study all came back at once, and prika's return is what finally lets the mint+prika Encore synergy matter in a real deck search. **Before measuring anything that reads `data/` from a worktree, run `python3 scripts/sync_worktree_data.py`** (no-op in main, copies only missing files, safe to re-run; `--check` reports drift and exits 1 without copying). Treat "the data isn't there" as a worktree artifact until checked against the main checkout. The same trap applies to `node_modules` and `.venv`.
- **lootandwaifus.com is the primary data source** (switched 2026-07-10) — at least as current as dotgg (which lags ~2 months of releases) and richer listing metadata. Cross-verified exact match against dotgg on Little Mermaid. `api.dotgg.gg` is now a fallback/cross-check. See `references/character-data-sources.md`.
- **A dotgg file's SKILL VALUES quietly go stale after a balance patch — the file looks fine, it's just wrong.** dotgg stopped updating around 2026-05 (see the weapon-stats decision), which is usually framed as "post-cutoff units are missing" — but pre-cutoff units that later receive a balance patch are a second, sneakier failure mode: the file still exists and still loads without error, it just holds the pre-patch numbers forever. Caught on Scarlet: Black Shadow after her 2026-07 patch: dotgg's level-10 Skill 1 reads 250.47/500/750.47% and Burst Charge Damage 150.12%, while lootandwaifus (re-collected, byte-identical to a fresh fetch) has the patched 283.03/565/848.03% and 169.63%. **Rule: encoding manifests default to `source=lootandwaifus` always, and this is mandatory (not just preferred) for any unit patched after 2026-05; dotgg files are trusted ONLY for the 6 weapon-stat fields (weapon/maxAmmo/damage/reloadTime/chargeTime/chargeDamage), never skill percentages.** lootandwaifus itself doesn't carry weapon stat numbers (only weapon type), so it can't confirm whether a patch changed those — verify weapon-stat changes against patch notes, not by diffing sources (Fienn confirmed via patch notes that SBS's 2026-07 patch left weapon stats unchanged).
- **A skill's own text can literally omit the token a later encoding needs — that's a second, distinct reason to migrate a manifest off dotgg, separate from post-patch drift.** `rapi-red-hood` moved from dotgg to lootandwaifus (2026-07-19, commit `f52b7cd`) not because any dotgg value had gone stale (every value carried over unchanged, only slot numbering shifted), but because dotgg's abbreviated skill text has no slot at all for Power of Inheritance's Stage 1 self burst-cooldown-reduction value — the B1-variant seat (`rapi-red-hood-b1`) needed exactly that value, and lootandwaifus' fuller per-level text carries it (`power_of_inheritance`'s new `description_value_02`). Worth checking for on any future manifest that needs a value the current source's text doesn't literally contain, not just when `check_skill_value_drift.py` flags a stale number.
- **Skill values are per level; retain all levels.** Encoding builders take a single-level values dict, so the user's actual skill level selects `levels[level-1]`. Users invest to different levels (Privaty's burst was used at level 7, not 10), so data collection must keep the full level data, never just max.
- **Signature weapon = `dollskills`, and it can add effects.** When `dollskills` is present the character's signature weapon is completed; it changes values and can add entirely new effects/slots, not just bigger numbers. Always check `dollskills` vs `skills`.
- **max_ammo changes apply to base ammo and sum.** An overload ammo increase and a skill's ammo decrease (e.g. Privaty EX Magazine) both compute against BASE ammo and add — the decrease is not taken off the already-increased total. See `attack_rate`.
- **dotgg character-data filenames use dotgg's own slug, not this project's roster slug — locate a unit's file by its JSON `"url"` field, never by constructing a filename from our slug.** Drake's dotgg file is `char_drake-nikke.json`, not `char_drake.json`; a naive filename join silently "not found"s (wrongly excludes) any unit whose dotgg slug diverges from ours. See `backend/app/skill_values.py::_dotgg_path_for`, `docs/decisions.md` ("dotgg data files are indexed by their JSON 'url' field, never by filename").
- **dotgg stopped updating its NIKKE data around 2026-05 — units released after that cutoff need a manual weapon-stats stub (`"source": "manual"`), and an unfilled field in that stub must be OMITTED, never left as an empty string.** `user_roster._weapon_stats` only checks field presence (`field not in dotgg_data`), not value validity — an empty string passes that check and then crashes at `int("")`/`float("")` deeper in the same function, whereas an absent field returns `None` and cleanly excludes the unit (no data yet, not a crash). `scripts/collect_dotgg_weapons.py --stub <slug>` templates leave TODO fields absent (tracked via a `_todo` list) for exactly this reason. See `backend/app/user_roster.py::_weapon_stats`, `docs/decisions.md` ("dotgg's NIKKE data feed stopped updating...").
- **ShiftyPad's tabs (Equipment/Skill/Collection/Cube) are `v-if`, not `v-show` — only the active tab's markup exists in the DOM, and the main stat panel is a SEPARATE DOM branch from all of them, so there is no single container that holds everything.** A pre-implementation manual spike clicked through the tabs and saw them appear to coexist (the SPA caches recently-viewed tab state), which misled the first capture design into assuming one `page.content()` snapshot on the Equipment tab would carry every surface (this claim is still stale in `tools/collect-blablalink/RECIPE.md` section (b) — the shipped `capture.js` overrides it). The robust capture instead visits each of `Equipment`/`Skill`/`Cube` in turn and plucks each surface (stat rows / overload / skill rows / cube) by its own local DOM anchor (e.g. the div whose header child is exactly `Equipment Effects`; a `div` with two `<p>` children labeled HP/ATK/DEF), deduping the stat-panel fragment which repeats on every tab. Skill content in particular only renders after the Skill tab is actually opened, on some units. See `tools/collect-blablalink/capture.js::pluckSurfaces`/`captureUnit`.
- **jsdom's `textContent` has no layout newlines, so `\bATK\b`-style regexes fail against concatenated stat text like `"ATK240903"` — locate the stat panel structurally, not lexically.** `parse.js` finds two-`<p>` rows whose value cell matches `"<number> <signed delta>"` (e.g. `"418862 -275319"`, `"5097 +89718"`) — this pattern is LV-position-independent (works regardless of which level the page happened to render at capture time) and, critically, is what DISTINGUISHES the main stat panel (always has a delta, because the level slider is always off the actual level after stepping to 400) from equipment/cube stat displays (always a bare number, no delta). `raid400 = actual + delta`; delta is negative when the account's real level is above 400 (the common case) and positive when a level-1 uninvested unit is stepped UP to 400.
- **Two ID systems must be joined via a third, rotating-URL source: the game API keys by `name_code`, ShiftyPad's URL keys by `resource_id`.** Join path: the nikke **directory** (a CDN JSON array carrying both `resource_id` and `name_code`, plus `original_rare` and `name_localkey.name` = English name; its URL hash rotates, so discover it at runtime by intercepting whichever JSON response on a ShiftyPad page load is an array whose items have both fields) × `GetUserCharacters` (the owned-roster API, keyed by `name_code`), filtered to `original_rare === 'SSR'` (raid content is SSR-only). **`GetNikkesOrder` is a favourites list only — it is NOT the owned roster and must not be used for enumeration**, a trap because its name suggests otherwise. See `tools/collect-blablalink/RECIPE.md` section (c)/(d), `collect.js`.
- **A name-alias table SHARED by two import paths with different naming conventions is a latent mis-mapping bug — and a silent one, because a wrong slug is still a *valid* slug.** The ExiaInvasion export uses short in-game names, the collector's `roster.json` uses full directory names, and both went through one `resolveSlug` alias table, so the two paths wanted contradictory mappings for the same string. Nothing errored: base `"Soline"` simply resolved to `soline-frost-ticket`, and since drafts merge by `character_slug`, the variant's accurate collected stats got clobbered by the base's. Fixed by giving the collector path its own `resource_id`-keyed table (`frontend/src/lib/resourceIdSlugMap.ts`) and leaving the alias table untouched for ExiaInvasion. Name-based schemes also can't separate the three distinct units that all display as `"Rei"`. See `docs/decisions.md` ("Collector roster units map to slugs by `resource_id`...").
- **When two encodings of the same character share ONE game ID, that ID cannot be the discriminator — identity keys answer "which character", investment/progression state needs its own layer.** A "dual-slot" unit (both `<slug>` and `<slug>-signature` encoded — Drake, Julia) has one `resource_id` for both; whether the signature applies depends on the user owning the in-game Favorite Item, which is per-user and changes over time. So `resourceIdSlugMap` maps id → BASE slug only, and ownership lives in a separate `SIGNATURE_OWNED` set. Merging the two into one table looks tidier and is wrong for everyone but the author, at one moment in time.
- **A guard test can only catch what its source of truth knows — name the blind spot as part of the guard's contract.** `backend/tests/test_resource_id_slug_map.py` cross-checks the hand-authored frontend map against the live `ENCODED_SLUGS`, so it catches typos, stale slugs, missing entries, and newly encoded dual-slots (encoding a new Nikke red-fails the backend suite until the map or `KNOWN_UNMAPPED` is updated — intentional). It cannot catch a wrong-but-VALID `resource_id`, because no committed data maps ids to names; closing that needs a committed public directory snapshot — **which now exists**, see the next bullet.
- **The public half of a session-gated source can often be committed even when the personal half cannot — splitting them turns "needs a live run to verify" into a normal test.** The nikke directory (194 units: `resource_id`/`name_code`/English name/rarity) arrives on the same authenticated ShiftyPad load as the roster, but carries no account data, so `collect.js --directory` dumps it to a committed `nikke-directory.json` while `roster.json` stays gitignored. The mode returns *before* the `game_openid` lookup, so it needs no account at all. `backend/tests/test_resource_id_directory.py` then checks each mapped slug against the directory's English name (normalised), closing the wrong-but-valid-id hole above — verified by injecting the exact bug class (Soline's base 71 in place of variant 74): the new guard names it, the old guard stays green. Two design notes that make the check honest: (a) the rule was derived from data, not assumed — 51 of 57 entries matched normalised-equality exactly, and the 6 that didn't were all collab units whose ShiftyPad name is a short form (`Ada` vs `ada-wong`), so they became an explicit `SLUG_NAME_EXCEPTIONS` table; (b) each exemption is **pinned to the exact directory name** it may differ from, so an exemption cannot widen into hiding a real mis-mapping. A side effect worth knowing: ids no longer require someone to *own* the unit, which is what let `jill-valentine` be mapped from evidence (841) rather than guessed, emptying `KNOWN_UNMAPPED`.
- **`data/dotgg/` and `data/lootandwaifus/` are gitignored local data assets — a git worktree does not get them for free.** Data-dependent tests fail broadly in a fresh worktree until both directories are copied over from the main checkout; conversely, deliverables produced while working in a worktree (new/updated data files) are handed off by copying them back to the main checkout, not by committing them (they're gitignored everywhere).
- **ShiftyPad's public share-URL uid's leading number is a ShiftyPad REGION id, not the API's `nikke_area_id` — a plausible-looking decode is a false lead.** `blablalink.com/shiftyspad?uid=<base64>` decodes to `<shiftypad_region_id>-<intl_open_id>` (e.g. `29080-1234567890123456789`). Calling `GetUserCharacters`/`GetUserCharacterDetails`/`GetUserProfileOutpostInfo` with `nikke_area_id=29080` (the uid's own leading number) returns `code 1303001` ("param invalid") — 29080 is unrelated to the game API's area. Cost one round-trip to discover; don't trust a decoded uid's leading number without checking the API's actual response code first. **The uid does not carry the account's region at all** — see the region-id entry below for where the real `nikke_area_id` comes from.
- **`nikke_area_id` is the game SERVER REGION, and 81 is Japan — not "international".** The authoritative list is `GET https://api.blablalink.com/api/lip/direct/commodity/Game/GetRegionList?game_id=29080`, which returns `area_list`: **81 Japan · 82 NA · 83 Korea · 84 Global · 85 SEA**. This project hardcoded 81 everywhere and called it "the international server" for weeks; that survived only because both accounts it was ever tested against were Japan-region. The first Korea-region account broke it: `GetUserCharacters` with area 81 for a KR `open_id` fails `code 1303002` ("proxy.GetUserShiftyspadPrivacy error") even when the target's ShiftyPad visibility IS public — the privacy record is looked up in the wrong region, so a region mismatch is indistinguishable from a privacy problem by its error message alone. **Lesson: a constant that happens to work for every sample you have is not a constant.** The value was only discoverable from blablalink's own region-list endpoint, not from the share URL, not from the account, and not by inference.
- **A failing first call can be reported as a failure of the SECOND one.** The bookmarklet's `GetUserCharacters` result is read as `(await call(...)).characters||[]`, so when that call is the one that breaks, the `||[]` flattens it into an empty roster and the NEXT call (`GetUserCharacterDetails` with `name_codes: []`) is what raises the visible error — `param invalid`, pointing at the wrong endpoint and the wrong parameter. Diagnosing it required a probe that reported every call's own `code` instead of stopping at the first throw. **When an error names a step, check that the step before it actually succeeded** — a defaulting `||` between two calls will hide the real one.
- **A caller-side authenticated blablalink session can read ANY user's roster/investment data once that user sets ShiftyPad visibility to public — the target's own credentials are never needed, but the call still isn't anonymous.** From one account's logged-in session, `GetUserCharacters`/`GetUserCharacterDetails`/`GetUserProfileOutpostInfo` for a DIFFERENT (public) account all return `code 0` with the full per-unit investment payload (`arm_equip_*`/`attractive_lv`/`harmony_cube_*` — exactly what `backend/app/stat_assembly.py`'s calculator consumes) and account-wide corporation-research rows — these are the same three endpoints `tools/collect-blablalink/collect.js`'s `fetchOwned`/`collectDetails` already call, just pointed at a different `open_id`. A session-less (anonymous) call to the same endpoints is refused with `code 300001` ("game not login") — so this is consent-gated by the target's visibility toggle, not by session ownership, but it is NOT a truly anonymous public API; some valid session is required, just never the target's. See `docs/decisions.md` ("Phase 7 Stage 1 reconnaissance: cross-account roster sync IS feasible...").
- **An ownership proof binds an `open_id` to a user by having them edit a cross-account-readable game-profile field — but NIKKE's game profile has no free-text bio, so the free channel is the showcase team, not a pasted code.** For a "first sync proves ownership, later re-syncs don't" flow (the abuse it stops: submitting someone else's public share URL as your own — false attribution / mass harvest of public rosters). The real endpoint is `GetUserProfileBasicInfo` (NOT `...BaseInfo`; guessed names return `code 220000` "not permission"), which returns cross-account `nickname` / `lv` / `profile_team` (5 showcase slots as `{name_code, slot}`) / `icon_id` — dumped in full against a public account, there is NO intro/comment/signature free-text field. So the two editable+readable proof channels are `nickname` (free text, high entropy, but a NIKKE nickname change costs currency) and `profile_team` (free to edit). The showcase team is the better channel precisely because we already read the user's owned roster: the challenge can be "put these N units you own into slots 1..N in this order," which is free, always performable, high-enough entropy, and proves in-game access — no reliance on a bio field edenpj's precedent implied but this API doesn't expose (edenpj likely read the community profile or the nickname). Endpoints observed on a shared-ShiftyPad load also include a whole community/guild layer under `/api/ugc/...` and `/api/lip/...` (distinct from `/api/game/proxy/Game/...`), some of which return the SESSION user's data rather than the viewed profile's — don't assume every endpoint on that page is keyed to the profile being viewed.
